ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Budget and Public Finance, I have the honour to present a report on the work of our committee on the Law amending the Customs Law and certain other laws. Our committee examined this law at its meeting on 10 March 2021. This law was adopted by the Polish Parliament on 25 February. During the committee meeting, we discussed the contents of this law very honestly and carefully... [[Talking in the O.R.]] [[Please pay attention to the report.]] Many comments have been made in our report as amendments to this law. Our Committee on Budget and Public Finance recommends that the High House adopt the 9 amendments adopted by the Committee in print No 334 A. All amendments accepted by the committee were supported by a representative of the government. This law amends the Customs Act, the Law on Enforcement in Administration, the Law on toll motorways and the National Road Fund, the Law on the Rules of Registration and Identification of Taxpayers and Taxpayers, the Tax Order, the Law on Environmental Protection, the Act on Goods and Services, the Law on the Railway Fund, the Act on Excise Tax, the Act on Gambling, the Act on National Tax Administration, the Act on the Development Fund for Bus Transport of Public Interest and the Act on Electronic Service. As you can see, this bill, which is not volumetricly huge, is updating a lot of laws. It must be said here that most of the provisions of this law simplify administrative procedures, tax procedures, customs procedures. I think that this is a law that will, to a large extent, and perhaps even fully benefit taxpayers. The primary objective of the amendment is to harmonise the procedure for the customs and treasury office's decision on the level of customs and tax duties and fuel levy and import levy. In the current legal situation in the case of imports of goods subject to customs and tax duties, the procedure for determining customs duties and tax duties Even though they refer to the same facts, They are run separately. Since, in most cases, tax decisions are issued after a customs decision has been taken, it is not possible to conduct tax and customs proceedings in parallel. This has a negative impact on the speed, efficiency and economics of the procedure. As separate proceedings are carried out in respect of the same facts, this results in duplication of procedural proceedings and the need to issue several dimensional decisions on a single customs declaration. It also leads to the fact that the entire procedure is, of course, prolonged, unnecessarily extended. The bill in the submission, which you will be adopting in a moment, eliminates customs-tax duality and harmonises the procedure relating to import duties, introducing solutions for one procedure and issuing one decision on import-related matters. The consequences of harmonising customs and tax procedures are changes in the relevant laws, which I will not mention here. In addition, the legislator simplifies the process of applying for inclusion in the list of guarantors entitled to provide guarantees lodged as a security to cover amounts arising from customs debts and to include customs agents in the list. It also changes the body holding the list of guarantors: it will be the head of the National Tax Administration instead of the Minister responsible for public finances. The Act also clarifies the provisions on customs proceedings, in particular by extending the scope of the Law of 29 August 1997 to these matters. This is an extension, among others. for the correction of accounting errors and other obvious errors in appeals, for the provision of non-compliant information and for the remission of proceedings. The Act also harmonises the type of customs-treasure checks to be carried out in respect of compliance with customs and tax law in relation to the carriage of goods in circulation between the customs territory of the European Union and third countries. The amendment of the Act on toll motorways and on the National Road Fund is also intended to ensure full consistency of the provisions governing the fuel levy with the provisions of the Act on excise duty, which implemented the provisions of the Council Directive of 16 December 2008 on general rules on excise duties in the Polish legal order. This directive has repealed earlier directives governing this scope of cases and, therefore, this implementation, following the entry into force of that law, of that directive, is valid and justified. The amendments were also made in the Enforcement Act in the administration and are intended to enable the enforcement of the issue fee in the administrative execution procedure. The amendment of the Act on the rules of registration and identification of taxable persons and payers will enable the issuing of a PESEL number for tax purposes to a natural person who is not engaged in a business activity or who is not a registered taxable person with a tax on goods and services which does not have a registration on the territory of the Republic of Poland. This provision is extremely important, important for people or foreign workers working in Poland. There is a change in the law on gambling. The amendments to this law aim to deformise, simplify and shorten the procedure for dealing with gambling cases. The novel also aims to limit access to information about illegal casinos and gambling in search engines and to prevent circumvention of rules on illegal gambling advertising. This is to be done by optimising the procedure for entering domains in the register for offering gambling not in accordance with the Gambling Act, as well as the procedures for punishing telecommunications traders and payment service providers that do not comply with the provisions of this Act. The proceedings in these cases will be transferred from the central level to the national tax administration. On this occasion, during the discussion on the amendment of the Gambling Act, there were doubts as to the fact that some of the provisions proposed by the Government and adopted by the Sejm change such very strict and restrictive provisions adopted in the amendment of the Gambling Act in the year of probably 2017 in the 17th or 18th, in the 17th. One, among others. they assumed that in the place where the illegal gambling salons were run, for several years, or more specifically for 5 years, it was not possible to run legal gambling points. It was to discourage, so to speak, all those who ran such an illegal salon of the game and also to include a certain responsibility for the legality of the property owners' undertaking, who should be responsible to some extent for the problem of who and for what purposes rents the room from them. We were wondering in the Senate, in committee, if, however, we should introduce even a short withdrawal period. I suggested that this be an annual penalty, calculated from the time it was determined that an illegal gambling salon was run in a given premises, so that for a year in the same place it would not be possible to run a gaming salon. However, in the end, I have not tabled this amendment. We have asked the Minister to address these doubts in her report or in her information. Well, the aim of the Gambling Act of 2009, however, was to prevent such illegal trading, such illegal activities as was often carried out in gambling salons. Such doubts have arisen. They also concerned This was raised during the discussion at the committee meeting of the time of storage of this material, which the owner of the arcade is obliged to record. The material relating to this activity must be stored for a reasonable period of time, and the Act shortens the period of storage of these materials. And that would be the most important changes that have been made to this law, which we discussed at the committee meeting. We have made some amendments and we propose to the High House that they be adopted. Finally, I would like to say that, as a rule, the Act will enter into force on the first day of the month following the end of the month following the date of publication. Some provisions will come into force even later. The Commission is pleasantly surprised here, pleased, because it is not often that such laws are so long, normal vacathio legis. But that's a good sign of how we're going to proceed over this bill. I therefore thank and recommend that the bill be adopted together with the amendments adopted by the committee. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u8	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, senators! As the senator said in the report of the committee's meeting, they are, in fact, aimed primarily at simplifying the current procedures for conducting customs procedures and tax proceedings in the case of imports of goods, but also at harmonising the fuel levy rules. Other solutions are also beneficial for entrepreneurs. Indeed, an element of this change is also the amendment of the law on gambling. As requested by the senator, I would like to address the issue of shortening this five-year period of banning the placement of gaming salons in places after the illegal gaming salons found. I would also like to reassure your senators. When we introduced this 5-year withdrawal provision on the placement of legitimate slot arcade games in premises that were rented for illegal activities, our aim was to prevent players from associating legal salons with illegal ones. That was the intention of the legislator at the time. After a few years of functioning of this provision, adopted, as mentioned by the senator, in 2017, the analysis of existing solutions led us to the conclusion that, in fact, the further reduction of the grey area in the field of gambling on slots, including above all providing players with protection against the negative effects of gambling, it is necessary to abolish this ban. We'll never really lead to... Probably we'll never have to deal with such a situation that players will stop using slots, stop playing. Thus, in order to protect them and protect society, it is necessary to direct this activity to legal gaming salons, i.e. to places where the player is protected, where it is monitored to play games where it is possible for the player to assert his rights in relation to objections, complaints or claims to court, and above all the protection of minors is ensured. So that from the point of view of limiting the gray zone, as well as ensuring the protection of these players, it is much more advantageous to run legal salons. This restriction should therefore be abolished. Plus, this withdrawal period, which is, I'd say, it's time to encrypt yourself that there was an illegal salon before, now we have a legal... Well, sometimes it's hard for a player to tell what kind of living room he's dealing with, and all the more difficult is a landlord who sometimes just doesn't know, unconsciously decides to rent his place. So that in relation to these tenants it will be a kind of protection of their rights. Time at which the illegal salon will be liquidated as a result of the activities of the National Tax Administration This procedure lasts for a limited period of time. The tenant will take the appropriate decision and procedures will be carried out to allow the opening of a legal salon, i.e. checking operations, official activities in relation to this already legal salon, i.e. a period of at least several months. So that it is not that one day at a time there is a change of activity in a given place. The second questionable element of this law is the shortening of the period of storage of audio-visual material from gaming salons on slots from 3 years to 12 months. I would like to calm down and say that, in the case of arcades for games on slots, basic evidence and in the context of tax, and in the context of the regularity of fulfilling the obligations and ensuring the security of these games on slots, there is a record from a particular game device, such a technical record recording a particular game. An audio-visual record is a record from the room in which these games are conducted, and it is an additional material, you can say, evidence. Consideration of the customer's opposition in the case of games on slots is the term approx. 3 months. So this period of 12 months is definitely sufficient here. This is simply the abolition of such an additional bureaucratic obligation, and this applies only to arcades on slot machines. This does not apply to casinos. There the situation looks different, due to the many types of games run at the casino it is the audio-visual material that is the basic control evidence. Nothing changes here, this duty, so to speak, takes much longer. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u15	Thank you very much, Senator, for that question. I'm explaining. Right now, this five-year ban only applies to legal... In general, this provision concerns legal activities. In relation to illegal... You can't drive illegal at all. There is no recipe that says: you can run illegal arcade games on slots. You can't, it's forbidden. The rule that is now in force says this: if, as the National Tax Administration, we find that illegal activities were carried out at a given point, i.e. there were unregistered slots, then it is not possible to open legal ones in such a place for 5 years. ..run by a company which carries out a monopoly of a State which has all the permissions to do so. It's a game slot machine. But this recipe doesn't say that you can't open illegal activities there for five years. Because by definition, illegal activities must not be opened up there. And we don't record all those places and all the places that are rented for different purposes, because there's no way. This provision starts to work when the National Tax Administration states: there is illegal activity, and we eliminate this illegal activity. And right now, this recipe says, for five years, you can't open legal activity there. And this is unfavorable from the point of view of market ordering and protecting players, because it is better in such a place to allow the opening up of legal activity as soon as possible and as if to take away the possibility or the desire of illegal entities to once again, once again, try to locate themselves in such places again. So this is a different kind of philosophy. I would also like to say that the additional provisions accompanying this, sanctions, i.e. penalties, which concern the placement of illegal slots, have led to such a situation that, fortunately, in the case of legal activities, i.e. e.g. gastronomy, small premises or gas stations, these machines have already disappeared from there. But of course, they appear and function so strictly. illegal slots points, which we are gradually trying to combat, whether by eliminating or operating. The point is that in those places where such illegal activities have been carried out, it should not lead to the situation of this withdrawal for 5 years, because by that time a legitimate monopoly cannot enter and occupy the premises and thus make this place one of the places of the games that are supervised by the State, by the law, by the appropriate instruments.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u27	Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. High Izbo! The amendment of the Act of. Bank Law of the Sejm adopted on 25 February, 26 February the Marshal of the Sejm passed the text of the Act to the Senate. Mr. Marshal sent this text to the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance. Our committee dealt with this law on 10 March. Now I would like to present a brief report on this law. First of all, I would like to say that the committee is asking for a law without amendments. The Act implements the Directive and Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union concerning the extension of supervision of the financial market and capital requirements to financial institutions in its national legal order. These changes are being implemented in response to the financial crisis that started in 2007 and 2008. The amendments introduced are of a technical nature and relate, inter alia, to: take into account to a greater extent the risks to institutions that trade on a large scale securities and derivatives, the new requirements for total loss-covering capacity under which they will be required to have increased loss-covering and recapitalisation capacity or to approve the financial activities of holding companies. The Act also introduced a number of other amendments. It is therefore necessary to amend the following laws: of course, in the act "Bank law', but in addition in the Act on the functioning of cooperative banks, their association and the banks', in the Act on Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, in the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, in the Law on Financial Market Supervision, in the Act on Macroprudential Supervision of the Financial System and Crisis Management in the Financial System, in the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities, in the Act on the Bank Guarantee Fund, in the system of guaranteeing deposits and compulsory restriction. The bill isn't controversial. As I said before, the only element that triggered the discussion at the committee's meeting was the letter of the Polish Bank Association, which contained a suggestion. In the view of the representatives of the banking sector, the provision should be added on the possibility of extending the time to submit a capital protection plan from 5 to 10 days with the approval of the Financial Supervision Authority. It turns out that such a provision is contained in another point of this law and therefore the committee did not have to amend it. One more thing I would like to say is that the law does not affect the public finance sector, including the state budget, as well as the budgets of local government units. Once again, on behalf of the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance, I would like to request the adoption of a law without amendment. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u28	Thank you very much, senator. At present, senators may submit no more than a minute of inquiry to the Senator rapporteur. ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u29	Thank you, Mr. Marshal. High Izbo! The proposed law amending the Law on Banking Law and certain other laws aims at implementing the European Union's rules on capital requirements for financial institutions. This is a revision of the CRD and the CRR, so-called. The amount to be reported in column 060 of this row shall be reported in column 060 of this row. This package is a continuation of the reform carried out by the Basel Committee and the European Union, which was prepared in response to the financial crisis in 2007. In 2015, the provisions implementing the provisions resulting from the CRD IV and the CRR, which were intended to comprehensively regulate the activities of banks and investment firms, inter alia. as regards supervision and capital requirements. Directive CRD V, the implementation of which is provided by the law, aims to take into account issues which have proved not sufficiently clear in the course of the practical application of the CRD IV Directive and have therefore been the subject of divergent interpretations, e.g. supervisory authorities, or which have been found to be unduly burdensome or too mild for some institutions. Main changes resulting from the CRD V Directive require Member States to introduce rules on the approval of financial activities of mixed financial holding companies and mixed financial holding companies, as well as direct supervisory powers in respect of such entities. These provisions were introduced to impose direct liability on holding companies to ensure that consolidated prudential requirements are met. The justification for these regulations is the fact that financial holding companies and mixed financial holding companies may be parent undertakings of banking groups. I would add that financial holding companies are currently not subject to financial supervision. The second major change is the introduction of a capital group in a third country of an intermediate EU parent undertaking where at least 2 institutions established in the Union are part of the same group and the total value of assets in the European Union is at least EUR 40 billion. This solution will enable the relevant financial market supervisor in one of the Member States to effectively control the European activities of the group. Another amendment is the provisions implementing the principle of proportionality in relation to reporting on the definition of categories of institutions and workers exempt from the obligation to apply certain elements of remuneration policy or the possibility of a simplified standard methodology for assessing interest rate risk by small and unsubscribed institutions. This means that smaller banks, with a relatively simpler business model, will be more subject to the principle of proportionality, which will in particular translate into smaller reporting requirements, disclosure of information to large banks and thus reduce their operating costs. In order to implement the provisions of the Directive, the Act also introduces provisions conferring powers on the Financial Supervision Commission to dismiss a member of the supervisory board or the management board of a bank, a financial holding company or mixed financial holding company and a member of the management board of a brokerage house where the person concerned does not fulfil the requirements necessary to perform the function in question. In addition, a change in the use of macro-prudential instruments has been introduced, i.e. the flexibility of the system risk buffer mechanism to establish a buffer for all exposures or a specific subset of exposures. The Act also provides for the revision of the maximum level of buffer of another systemically important institution from 2 to 3% and the introduction of an additional method for identifying the buffer of global systemically important institutions. Although the latter institutions currently do not exist in Poland, Poland is obliged to implement the provisions relating to such entities. As the rapporteur said, in the Senate Finance Committee, this law raised no greater doubts, apart from short questions concerning legislative issues and doubts of the Polish Bank Association, which have been clarified. This project also raised no doubt in the Lower House, i.e. the Sejm. I would also like to inform you that, as part of the amendments made in the Lower House, amendments have been made to draw up annual reports and consolidated annual reports for 2020, and this is a provision worth paying attention to for two reasons. Firstly, because this is not strictly due to the implementation of EU rules, and this is the only provision that is not pure implementation, but is, colloquially speaking, a national addition. But secondly, and this is important, this is a provision that a number of entrepreneurs are looking forward to producing such annual reports. So that this change is clearly beneficial for entrepreneurs and also expected by such entrepreneurs. I therefore ask the High House to adopt this law. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u66	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to strongly recommend to the High House the adoption of this law. It contains a package of several solutions that respond to the notified national tax administration's Ministry of Finance's negative developments on the excise trade market. Designed changes will improve the operating conditions of honest companies and make it much more difficult for companies that violate the law. It is not a coincidence that this law also works with us, in the Ministry of Finance, under the name of the excise package. It operates under this name because it contains sealing regulations for the vast majority of excise goods. It concerns the marketing of contaminated ethyl alcohol, energy products, tobacco drought, fuel and shipping fuels and passenger cars brought to Poland. This is a step that will close the process of electronicising excise clearance that has been underway for several years. From next year As it has been for several years in the case of VAT settlements In the case of excise duties, businesses will also make declarations no longer on paper and electronically. Entrepreneurs will also keep records electronically. In this way, employees of the National Tax Administration will have direct automatic access to information on products, excise goods produced by market operators and traded, which will also enable automatic analysis of turnover and rapid detection of possible irregularities. A major change will also be the involvement of trade inspections in the process of monitoring the quality of fuels in terms of their origin. The commercial inspection will examine the quality of the fuel not only with regard to its, well, quality and driving characteristics, but above all also with regard to whether it contains trace amounts of dyes indicating that the fuel was not intended for vehicles, for cars, only previously intended simply for other purposes, e.g. heating purposes, whether it was created as fuel for other means of transport, means for which fuel is taxed at a lower rate, as the senators have rightly noted here. The regulation will also implement a solution which will significantly impede the authorisation of excise goods trading by entities cooperating with tax offenders, often by so-called. poles. These are often people who use stolen IDs, sometimes homeless or unemployed. Changes in the law will make it easier to refuse an excise permit when it is identified that the applicant will not pay excise duties, e.g. where there is no means or experience that would allow him to carry on his declared activity. Here I will immediately refer to the question which came up with regard to the Senator of the rapporteur. Of course, we are here within the framework of the tax administration, so that the decision in this regard will be subject to an appeal. Moreover, in the event of such a threat, the office shall request the trader, giving him the opportunity to replace within 30 days the person declared to direct the business relating to the trading of excise goods simply with another person, for whom such doubts do not arise. This means that we will not face immediate refusal on the side of the office, and with a certain Sanitation path, which in the event of rapid contact between the office and the entrepreneur will allow really quick replacement of the person to whom there are doubts, just another one. Mrs. Marshal, High Izbo, with all this in mind, I would kindly ask the High House to adopt the bill. Thank you.

Here I would like to stress very much that this phenomenon does not happen at all. And I would like to ask you very much to give them this information in their dealings with entrepreneurs as well. What's the proposal? Raising the maximum fine for tax offences, which may be imposed by means of a criminal mandate. What does that mean? Currently, the mandate can be imposed up to double the minimum wage, i.e. up to PLN 5 000 600. We want this limit to be raised more than twice or almost three times to PLN 14,000. What exactly does that mean? The framework of penalties will remain exactly the same as it was, but more cases can be dealt with by way of a mandate. And now: what is the practical consequence? Currently, fiscal services detect a lot of cases in which the perpetrator's offence of tax is obvious, e.g. in the event of the arrest of a person carrying excise goods across the border, the detention of a smuggler. If there is no controversy about the circumstances of the case, both the office and the detained want to close the case quickly. The National Tax Administration gives a mandate in such a situation and the perpetrator accepts it, pays it and the proceedings are closed. The problem raised to us and by the National Tax Administration, and by the courts, is a very large number of cases that could be closed by a mandate procedure if the penalty was simply higher. Hence, our proposal to raise almost three times, so that there will no longer be a situation at least to a new limit, that also the office, and the person herself, so to speak, caught at the border wants to pay a mandate, does not want to go to court, and although the case is completely unambiguous, is forced to do so by law. And I would like to remind you that, as a result, 14,000 cases of this kind go to court every year. If this volume could be halved, the speed of handling cases in courts and the efficiency of the administration and the judiciary would be improved in a tangible way. Let us remember that in such a situation the indictment must be written, the case ends with a judgment. Very often this is completely unnecessary. And in no way do we make things worse for those responsible for tax offences. If they do not agree with the position of the authority, they may not accept the mandate and refer the case to the court and, at the same time, give them the opportunity to deal quickly with the matter, an opportunity which they had not previously had. Hence it can be said that here we are dealing with a solution that taxpayers are asking us to do and which is really expected by them. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration on the Act adopted by the Sejm on 25 February 2021 amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments and certain other laws. On 2 March, the Senate Marshal sent the bill to the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration. The committee held its meeting on 10 March 2021. Following the review of the bill, the committee brings: The High Senate will approve the attached bill. This law is the implementation of the obligation resulting from the incorrect, in the opinion of the European Commission, transposition of the Directive of the European Parament and of the Council of 13 December 2011 on the assessment of the effects of certain public and private projects on the environment. 8 March 2019 European Commission in a reasoned opinion addressed to the Republic of Poland pursuant to Article. 258 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union indicated that both the provisions of the Act of 23 October 2008 on the provision of environmental information and its protection, as well as the provisions of certain other laws providing legal grounds for the granting of investment permits for projects which may significantly affect the environment are incompatible with the provisions of the European Parliament and of the Council of 13 December 2011 on the assessment of the effects of certain public and private projects on the environment as regards Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 3, i.e. rules on access to justice for the public concerned. In its reasoned opinion and then during its working meetings with representatives of the Government of the Republic of Poland, the European Commission pointed out that, in order to ensure effective access to justice, the public, when complaining of an administrative decision, must also be able to request the administrative court or an independent body to order a provisional measure so that certain measures may be interrupted and to prevent the resulting threat to human health or the environment by temporarily suspending the implementation of the permit for the contested undertaking. The European Commission also pointed out that, according to settled case-law of the Polish administrative courts, it is not possible to apply a provisional measure to an environmental decision, since it is an indirect decision and therefore the implementation of it does not pose a risk of causing significant damage. The European Commission also pointed out that NGOs are not party to investment authorisation procedures for certain projects, e.g. construction permits, water law permits or geological and mining concessions. In addition, the European Commission stressed that, in accordance with the article. 8 The results of the consultation and information collected pursuant to Article 8 of the Directive. This Directive must be taken into account in the procedure for granting investment authorisations. Where an investment permit does not adequately take into account the results of the environmental impact assessment or does not cover the conditions contained in the environmental decision, the public concerned, including environmental organisations, must be able to challenge the legality of such authorisation. Therefore, in order to ensure the correct transposition of the Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 3 of the Directive in question in national law must ensure that the parties concerned are able to appeal the investment authorisation decision and at the next stage It also refers the decision to the court and to the application for interim measures. The Government of the Republic of Poland has undertaken to amend existing legislation in order to eliminate identified infringements and possible non-compliance with Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 3 directives. Today's law aims to ensure that the public concerned can have full access to justice in the investment process for projects likely to have a significant impact on the environment. This law is divided into three parts. The first part amends the Environmental Information and Protection Act, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments. These changes are linked precisely to the introduction of an appeal and judicial procedure with regard to environmental decisions. Mechanisms have been put in place at these stages to enable parties and environmental organisations to request interim measures in relation to the suspension of the implementation of the environmental decision and to introduce a review and judicial procedure for specific investment decisions, allowing environmental parties and environmental organisations to challenge these authorisations for compliance with the environmental decision. In the second part of this law, i.e. in the article. Two hundred and twenty-five, amendments were made to 24 specific laws. These laws introduce an appropriate reference to the mechanisms designed in our Act, referring to the decision issued on the basis of those laws, which are defined in the draft as an investment permit. Additional substantive changes have also been made to these provisions in individual acts. These laws are not uniform in terms of those provisions which the European Union is calling into question as incompatible with the Directive, and therefore this amendment could not be made in bulk. The individual rules had to be amended because they differed from one another in some laws. And in the third part of the law, i.e. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: Transitional provisions are in place. It regulates the impact of this law on administrative and judicial proceedings that take place on the date of its entry into force. The purpose of the Act is to include its rules of procedure in progress by empowering the public concerned and non-governmental organisations to the greatest extent appropriate for the stage of the administrative and judicial procedure which will take place on the date of its entry into force. In view of the European Commission's claim to restrict judicial review in respect of investment decisions referred to in the special laws, the protection period after which the court cannot revoke the investment decision has been extended to 60 days. The Act fully abolishes the protection period in the event of non-compliance of an investment decision with an environmental condition decision or a decision issued after a re-assessment of the impact. The same shall apply to the procedure for annulment of that decision. In turn, in response to the European Commission's allegations that it is not possible for the public concerned to verify the compatibility of the investment decision with the environmental one, the Act provides that environmental organisations and parties to proceedings for the issue of an environmental decision should be entitled to appeal against investment decisions and then to actions before the courts in relation to the non-compliance of those decisions with the environmental decision. Mr Andrzej Schweda-Lewandowski, General Environment Director, stated that the draft law and each of the articles were agreed with the European Commission and developed in meetings with its representatives and that the European Commission was 100% It accepts this proposal and considers that it is compatible with EU law and, above all, meets all the directives and there is no doubt about it. The bill addressed to the Senate ultimately includes 41 pages and 29 articles. The entire law addressed to the Sejm, together with the justification, the public consultation report and its annexes, consisted of 113 pages. Ms Małgorzata Golińska, Secretary of State in the Ministry of Climate and Environment, Chief Conservation Officer of Nature; Mr Andrzej Szweda-Lewandowski, General Director of Environmental Protection; Ms Anna Jasińska, Director at the General Directorate for Environmental Protection together with colleagues; Mr Bartłomiej Zydel, Expert of the Polish Districts Union; Mr Marek Wójcik from the Association of Polish Cities; Bartosz Kwiatkowski from the Frank Bold Foundation; Ms Sylwia Szczutkowska and Radosław Ślusarczyk, representatives of the Association In a discussion that was conducted in a very factual and good atmosphere, which is undoubtedly due to the chairman of the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration, Mr Zygmunt Frankiewicz, both the senators and the mentioned representatives of institutions and organisations spoke. Senator Janusz Błucherz stated that this law belongs to such legal solutions as can be said to not satisfy everyone. There will be no wolf's full and the sheep's whole. It concerns information on projects that can have a significant impact on the environment and therefore society must have full knowledge but also an impact on their location and implementation. All those involved in investment processes or earlier know very well that this procedure is very complicated, often without justified reasons too slow, incomprehensible to many passive observers. Local authorities know this best, who are accounted for by the residents with the effectiveness of decisions made, rapid implementation of investments in voivodship, urban or municipal areas. However, regardless of what functions we perform, whether we are self-government, parliamentarians, entrepreneurs, heads of associations or environmental foundations or currently ruling in the country, we should bear in mind the principle of environmental priority over economic activity and the principle of non-distortion of the state of the environment. If we take these principles into account, we need to examine whether this law, which came to us from the Sejm, meets the requirements and requirements imposed by the European Commission in respect of the amended law. [[Secretary of State in the Ministry of Climate and Environment Małgorzata Golińska: Mr. Marshal, if I may, it's a short one.]] Here you are.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u129	Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. High Senate! By entering into what the Senator rapporteur of the two committees spoke about very comprehensively discussing the bill, but also in the discussion that has been taking place, I will allow myself not to repeat what the idea of drafting the bill is from. As the senator pointed out, it is a response to the reasoned opinion of the European Commission, which pointed out to us violations in the main law, namely the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments, as well as in some other laws, i.e. the so-called. Specialists. The Commission has pointed out to us that we should emphasise more strongly the access of the public concerned to justice. The EIA Directive, to which we adapt Polish law, ensures a wide public participation in decision-making, including access to appeal procedures, as well as access to justice in environmental matters for so-called. the public concerned. I say "so-called ', because it is clearly defined in the directive as a society which is or may be affected or has an interest in environmental decision-making procedures. For the purposes of this definition, environmental NGOs meeting the requirements of national law shall be deemed to have an interest in this respect. In our country, the general principles governing the participation of the public concerned, in particular environmental organisations and other social organisations and parties to proceedings, in decisions relating to the implementation of projects likely to have a significant impact on the environment, are governed by the Act on the provision of information, in short the EIA, as well as by the Code of Administrative Procedure and the law on proceedings before administrative courts. In addition, the laws governing the granting of investment permits contain provisions of the nature of lex specialis. It is about building law, water law, geological and mining law and specializations. The Polish government has committed itself to preparing a change of rules. This law, which we are discussing today, has been drafted with broad support and in cooperation with all the resorts that may be affected. The work was carried out by the Directorate-General for Environment as designated by the Minister for Climate and Environment by the authority empowered to carry out this work. Therefore, in case of specific questions, I will ask, Mr Marshal, for the support of the Directors of the Directorate-General for Environment present as those who directly participated in all the work, arrangements, but also discussions with the European Commission. As this law aims to ensure that the public concerned has as full access to justice as possible in the investment process for projects that can significantly affect the environment, we have introduced a number of solutions that extend the powers of the parties to conduct and environmental organisations vis-à-vis environmental decisions and extend the powers of those entities to follow-up decisions, i.e. investment decisions for which environmental decisions are required. On the one hand, our intention was to bring the law into line with the EIA Directive or to respond to the reasoned opinion of the European Commission, but on the other hand, it was also at our heart that these proposed solutions should not lead to the situation that it would be schematically possible to automatically block many investments. Hence, perhaps the discussions that the rapporteur spoke about today, and perhaps different views depending on whether the discussion took place at the meeting of the local government committee, in which long-term local government officials participated in the discussion, experienced in conducting various kinds of investments... This discussion was different from the discussions at the Environment Committee, where, however, the main objective of the discussion was to put certain issues as priorities by many non-governmental organisations. It is worth pointing out that in the Act, when we referred to the Commission's complaint that it was not possible to ask the Court for interim measures, we introduced the following solutions: we made it possible to apply for interim measures at the stage of the adoption of the environmental decision and made it possible to apply for interim measures at the stage of the adoption of the investment decision. With regard to the allegations regarding the limitation of judicial review in respect of investment decisions referred to in the special laws, we have extended the protection period after which the court cannot revoke the investment decision to 60 days. We have completely abolished the protection period in the event of non-compliance of investment decisions with the decision on environmental conditions or a decision taken after the re-assessment of the impact. On the other hand, in response to the allegations of failure to provide the public concerned with the possibility to control the compatibility of the investment decision with the environmental decision, we gave environmental organisations and parties the power to bring an appeal against the investment decisions and then complaints to the court regarding their non-compliance with the environmental conditions decision. As I said, it seems to us that, although we are not able to satisfy everyone, the proposed solutions address above all the question of compliance with the directive. But at the same time, by allowing or extending the powers of green NGOs, we do not give the opportunity to automatically block the economic development of our country. I think this is important in the context of the question that Senator Tyszkiewicz had just asked the rapporteur, but it is also important in the context that we have placed several fuses in the bill. We give non-governmental organisations far-reaching powers, but only after an analysis by an authority or by a court can the enforcement of an environmental decision be suspended. Why am I talking about this? Because one of the amendments that was adopted at the meeting of the Environment Committee is an amendment far beyond what we have proposed. One of the amendments explicitly states that the authority which considers the appeal against the decision shall, by suspending the proceedings, suspend the implementation of that decision if it has been granted immediate enforcement and that the authority responsible for reopening the procedure for the decision in the situation referred to in the preceding paragraph shall refrain from ex officio enforcement of that decision. And yes, the adoption of this amendment will implement the scenario that Senator Tyszkiewicz was afraid of. There will be the possibility of automatic blocking of investments. This is why not only this, but also other amendments tabled by the Senators' Group at the meeting of the Environment Committee, following the analysis that we have done with the Directorate-General for Environment, have been assessed by the Government negatively. I, too, today, ask the senators not to accept these proposed amendments so as not to lead to a situation where the solutions set out in the Act block us from making investments in our country. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u160	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Brief information on the purpose of this submission. It did not raise any doubts either in the Senate or in the Seym, but it is worth saying a few sentences about this law. That law is intended to implement the provisions of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2017/2205 of the European Commission of 29 November 2017 on detailed rules for the procedures for reporting commercial vehicles showing serious or dangerous deficiencies identified during the roadside technical inspection. That Regulation requires Member States carrying out roadside technical checks to notify without delay the results of the inspection of the contact point of the Member State of registration of the vehicle. This notification should be made using an exchange system for communicators This is the RSI system. In addition, the Regulation sets out procedures and minimum requirements for the content of RSI messages. Therefore, the proposed provisions of the Act assume that the exchange of RSI messages will take place on the basis of data collected in the Central Record of Violations. It is an electronic system operated by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport. The draft law introduces the obligation, firstly, to collect information on the VIN number of the controlled vehicle in the Central Register of Violations and, secondly, to provide information on the results of checks by the main road transport inspector to other European Union contact points competent for the country of registration of the vehicle during which serious or dangerous deficiencies have been identified using the RSI message exchange system. The Act shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. As I said at the beginning, it did not arouse great emotions during seym and senate work. I ask the High House to accept the bill submitted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u165	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I am very pleased and proud to present a report on the meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure held on 19 March 2021 on the Law amending the Road Traffic Law and certain other laws. Ladies and gentlemen, the law really concerns the regulation of scooters and scooters. Wait a minute, personal transport devices. The main task of this law That's what the Minister said. It was legal to regulate scooters, their speed, their departure to parking places and pedestrian safety. This bill is really needed. Some things need to be regulated so that there is no suffrage, but they need to be regulated, as it seems wise to find a compromise between the safety of electrical devices and the safety of other people on the way. The discussion was substantive because this case should not be political, security should not be political. The bill has quite a long history, but we are very happy that it has finally been created. ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u186	Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. High Izbo! I thank the senator for the precise definition of the changes that we propose in this law, the amendment of the law on road traffic. I, in addition, would like to highlight a few very important issues on which we have focused in a special way. The first is that we qualify electric scooters, but also other personal transport devices, as vehicles. At the moment, their status is not regulated, which is why Here I answer the question about statistics right away. The police do not carry out statistics on these devices, as the person who uses these devices is considered to be a pedestrian. So that all the events, all the road accidents that so far have occurred involving people moving on electric scooters or other personal transport devices, were included in the catalogue of events involving pedestrians. Since the entry into force of these provisions, electric scooter or other personal transport equipment will be treated as vehicles and then there will be a completely different eligibility of this type of event. The courts will also have no doubt that someone who was riding a scooter was using a vehicle to move and was not a pedestrian, as it has been for the past months. This is a fundamental issue because the vehicle user has certain obligations, of course there are rights, but also certain prohibitions. In this law they are very precise. The most important thing is... From my observations made especially here in Warsaw, a summer time when it was warm It appears that many people used these devices, electric scooters, under the influence of alcohol. It was visible at first glance. First of all, they were tourists who treated it as a fun, not necessarily an element of communication, a means of transport. This will be prohibited as from the entry into force of these provisions. So we don't let you drive after alcohol. We also do not allow two people to ride on one scooter, nor do we allow another vehicle to be dragged when using an electric scooter or another personal transport device, and these were the most often diagnosed by us situations that occurred in the past three years. With regard to the selection of the infrastructure that we allow, first of all And it's perfectly natural to allow whether we point out that an electric scooter or another personal transport device is used for cycling or cycling. If this is not the case, then the roadway, but subject to the speed limit, i.e. the maximum speed permitted on this section of the road 30 km/h, and if there is no cycle path or if the maximum speed is higher, then the pavement, but provided that 4 elements are met: firstly, driving at a speed close to pedestrian speed, secondly, giving way to pedestrian priority, thirdly, taking special care, fourthly, not hindering pedestrian traffic. So that all these 4 factors, all these 4 elements must be met together so that the user of the electric scooter can move not only legally, but also safely on the sidewalk, i.e. so that he can ensure this safety for himself, but above all for the safety of pedestrians. Because the overarching aim of the Ministry of Infrastructure has set itself before it is to create such regulations as to allow electric scooters to ride on the sidewalk, but precisely subject to the full protection of pedestrians, to allow pedestrians to enjoy safe, unfettered, unlimited use of pavement infrastructure. And in our view, this objective has been met in these provisions. The next question is the question of age. Why 10 years, not e.g. 12 or 14? Such proposals were made, but they were not from expert backgrounds, but from political backgrounds. I think one of the amendments in the Sejm talked about allowing the use of electric scooters to people since they were 12. However, we are in the position that a child, who is ten years old, has acquired a bicycle card, i.e. passed a course, training, whether at school or at a road centre, and has acquired some theoretical and practical skills regarding cycling, may also benefit from this means of transport. We don't see any contraindications here. Someone who is aware of safe movement, with proper behaviour on the road, can also use an electric scooter or a personal device. And let me remind you that, due to the lack of regulation, there are no such regulations, which at the moment theoretically even a six-year-old can use an electric scooter on the sidewalk, is treated as a pedestrian and no one can stop him from doing so. Of course, he can do so in the care of his parents. So that these are the fundamental issues that we had in the drafting of this law, this was a goal. The bill was consulted twice. The project was proposed by the Ministry of Infrastructure in June 2019. The first consultation brought a great deal of new proposals, comments and opinions, which were examined very strongly by the Ministry of Infrastructure. We thought that some elements were really worth including in the project, it was worth reorganizing. We did it and this bill was consulted for the second time. And then there were also many environments who expressed their opinion. I will admit that in the previous year, we did not run with this law on purpose, seeing that the pandemic situation was not conducive to the use of electric scooters. Some operators even withdrew during that summer season from making these scooters available in large cities. Well, the traffic was much smaller than in 2019. But we think that the situation in 2021 will be similar to that in 2019, so we want these rules to come into force with the new season. If, at this meeting, the High House adopts this law and it goes to the Sejm later, I think that the Sejm will deal with it on 30 March and the Act will enter into force 30 days after the publication in the Official Journal, which means that in the second half of May. And from then on, these new, necessary, expected rules will be in place, as you have already pointed out. Finally, I would like to assure you that we are not closing ourselves to other proposals. We will observe how this law works, we will observe what its effects are, whether it is fulfilling its role, whether it requires adjustments, changes and going with the spirit of time, with the spirit of modern technologies, which are everywhere, also when it comes to such individual communication. And if necessary, we are, of course, open to revision of these provisions. In addition, Western countries, in which these scooters were earlier and which had previously taken up this subject, also used this practice. First the first legislation, then the first amendment, if necessary, is the second amendment to go with the spirit of time and keep up with what is new on the market also in the road space. I thank the Committee on Infrastructure for its substantive discussion, thank you for the proposals, for the amendments. With regard to the amendment related to the lighting of pedestrian crossings, and with this deadline, which was used in this amendment, i.e. the term related to the area of construction, I will say that this term is not included in the Law on Road Traffic, but is contained in the Public Roads Act, and this article amends the Law on Public Roads. So it is the same term, similar to that which currently exists in the Polish legal order. Once again, thank you very much for your work in the committee, and of course, I would like to count on the second part of the substantive discussion. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u206	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report on the meeting of the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration, which took place on 4 March this year. At the meeting, the Act on the amendment of the Act on the National Census of Population and Housing in 2021 was examined. This law was adopted by the Sejm on 25 February 2021. The draft law was introduced by the Government. Some amendments were made during the work in the Sejm, but only of a more precise and technical and legislative nature. As for the substance of this law, there were no reservations. It was adopted by a majority: 312 votes in favour, 11 against, 129 abstentions. This Act aims to amend certain amendments to the Act of 9 August 2019 on the National Population and Housing Census in 2021. These changes aim primarily at extending the duration of the national census of the general population and housing in 2021, hereinafter referred to as the census, by 3 months. The census will run from 1 April 2021 to 30 September 2021 and not, as originally planned, until 30 June 2021. The second change is the shift in the period during which the follow-up and follow-up tests will be carried out. Those studies shall start on 1 October 2021 and shall be carried out until 31 December 2021. In addition, the Act contains provisions which harmonise the rates of remuneration or the census add-on for census rankings, clearly define the rules for determining remuneration or the census add-on for census rankings for conducting an interview, specify the resignation of additional functions of census counters who are employees of public statistics units and adapt the rules on free distribution, transmission of information by Polish Television and by Polish Radio. These are the main changes that concern the census of the general population and housing in 2021. As far as the discussion in committee is concerned, this law did not raise any objections. The legislator only paid attention to the time limits for the procedure of this law. Because we're going to the Senate now, and the committee has tabled one amendment, so if she's going back to the Sejm... If the Sejm were to meet on the 30th of this month and it is recorded that the Act enters into force on 1 April 2021, the President would not be required to do so constitutionally for 21 days, which is guaranteed to work on and sign it and followed by its announcement. That is why I will make the amendments that will complete this missing issue. As far as the committee meeting is concerned, one amendment has been tabled, quite significant, namely it has been deleted... This amendment includes the deletion of point (a) above. and in Article 4 (1) (a) of Regulation (EU) No 1308/2013, the following paragraph is added: Point 1 (9). This was unanimously adopted, even with the approval of the factors representing the government in the matter of this law. And this amendment is written down. ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u218	I am immediately in a hurry to answer the four questions asked by your senator. Why now? It was already in January that commissioners were appointed. No, as early as April of the previous year, at the beginning of the pandemic we completed the first trial census, in the autumn of last year we carried out a general agricultural census of more than 1.5 million farms. And there was no news here. What we are now changing in the law is obviously due to the length of the legislative process. We started already in December and it was prepared based on the experience we gained in the general agricultural census. Following the experience of a fully completed census with success, whose first results will be presented next week, we have come to the conclusion that, in the event of a third wave of pandemic, we would like to prepare for it in a special way, i.e. Extend the time we have to make a census using phone interviews. In the most extreme, critical case, we are prepared to carry out a census solely on the basis of online self-written and telephone interviews. Here I must add, in order to reassure the senator, as regards the concerns we have heard, that we carry out the census with a very modern method, so-called. mixed method. In fact, we make the most of the administrative sources we source from other administrations, validate them, verify them, compile them, and as a kind of complement, we also consider the possibility of conducting direct interviews. They are extremely important to us, because these source registers are associated with various types of problems, methodological inconsistencies, which are quite important for statistics. Hence we must also obtain other sources, but this source in the form of direct interviews is not the only and is not the primary source in the implementation of the inventory. This is also indirectly the answer to the question about the case of Germany. Yes, of course, it is true that the Germans moved the census to the following year, but it is true in such a context that the German census is a traditional census, implemented as we did 20 years ago, based solely on paper solutions. And under these conditions, they really are not able to carry out this census. Most countries, the vast majority of the countries that we know about, keep the inventory at that date, at which we implement it more or less, because they use more modern methods. There, this German statistical system has a kind of special solutions, the land has a very large voice, where carrying out such statistical tasks is far more complicated than in other countries. Therefore, they also have reasons why they organize it in this way. But there is no need for us to translate this inventory in connection with the limitations we have, and the delay would cause a significant loss to the knowledge we have, which is essential to us in this particular pandemic situation, in which we want to correctly estimate and monitor the course of socio-economic patterns, diagnose and analyse them in effect for the benefit of our society, for our country. And this is extremely important, because we are immediately able to translate this information into concrete results, concrete added value that we can bring to public discourse and to everything that we are doing together. Therefore, the answer to the next question concerning the value of these data is this: it is huge. It is precisely at this point in time when we are going through a pandemic that precise knowledge about local communities, about demographic structure, about housing resources is the basic knowledge needed to take any informed action optimized for future effects. And it seems that having the possibilities that we have, preparing this inventory for a long time in a very modern way... Indeed, we are one of the leading countries, I will not hide it, and often invited to present my experiences and share my experience in the field of public statistics in general, and from the censuses in detail And we're happy to do that, because that's how we see our role in this system that we're creating together. I think that's it. So I tried to answer these four questions.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u252	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Senate! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Committee on Education, Science and Sport, a report on the Law amending the Law If the senators allow, I will present the purpose and subject of the bill. First of all, this law aims to introduce a system for monitoring the careers of public graduates and non-public secondary schools. This monitoring is to be carried out to collect information on the careers of graduates of these schools. They will be collected for education policy at national, regional and, of course, local level. This is intended to enable the orientations and content of education to be adapted to the requirements of the labour market and to better prepare pupils for the occupation and further training. Monitoring is to be carried out annually and includes an analysis of the educational and professional situation of graduates in 3 stages: after one year, after two years and after five years after their graduation from secondary school. According to the provisions of this Act, monitoring will be carried out on the basis of data collected in the Educational Information System and the Integrated Information System about Higher Education and Science POL-on. It will also use data collected by the District Examination Boards as well as by the Social Insurance Institution. I'm talking about monitoring insured accounts or premium payer accounts. This monitoring will be carried out by the Minister responsible for Education and Education, and according to the regulations the Minister will be able to entrust this task to an organisational unit subordinate to him. The Institute of Educational Research in Warsaw will be responsible for analysing and developing the results of monitoring the careers of graduates. The results of this monitoring will be made available by the Minister responsible for Education and Education in an aggregated form on the relevant website of the Public Information Bulletin. Just a few more information. The monitoring of graduates' careers in 2021 and 2029 will also include graduates of public and private upper secondary schools who graduated from school in the school years 2018/2019 and 2023/2024. As a consequence of the introduction of a system for monitoring the careers of secondary school graduates, it is also necessary to amend the relevant laws, including the Social Security Act on the possibility of obtaining information to assess the situation of graduates on the labour market, as well as the Act on the Educational Information System to supplement the data collection catalogue in the education information system for conducting the monitoring of graduates' careers and to allow for obtaining specific data from the database of the educational information system by the Minister responsible for Education and Education, as well as the Law on Higher Education and Science in the Law on the possibility of obtaining information from the Integrated Information System on Higher Education and Science POL-on on continuing to study by graduates of technicians, industry-related secondary schools, general schools and university schools. The provisions on the monitoring of graduates' careers are to enter into force on the day following the date of publication. Ladies and gentlemen, at the meeting of the Committee on Science, Education and Sport, two questions were addressed to the Minister, which were, as if to clarify the provisions of the Act. There was no doubt that... well, there were doubts, but the Minister answered those questions. At the committee meeting, we unanimously decided that the committee was asking the Senate to adopt this law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-03-24-senat-22-1.u261	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the government's position on the draft... already at this moment a law passed after the third reading by the Sejm, currently under investigation in the Senate. The revised Law on the amendment of the Law and of certain other laws is another action planned in the framework of the vocational training reform implemented since September 2019. These changes bring vocational education closer to the labour market. The employer becomes a school partner. We have now prepared another mechanism that will allow for an even more accurate adjustment of education to the needs of the labour market. The draft law is a response to the lack of adequate, detailed and reliable information on the fate of secondary school graduates observed for years. The aim of the proposed changes is to provide feedback on the careers of secondary school graduates. This information will be used for educational policy at national, regional and local level. And so I answer the senator's question, but I can make it more specific if that question comes up later: there will be information in specific educational institutions about the fate of graduates in particular schools. To this end, the Act sets out new public tasks for the Minister responsible for Education and Education, i.e. monitoring the careers of graduates of all types of secondary schools, i.e. First and second degree professional schools, technicians, police schools, general education schools, special admission schools. The rules introduced refer to similar solutions adopted in the higher education system, but are adapted to the specifics of educational data. Graduate monitoring will be carried out every year. The research group will be all secondary school graduates who have completed their studies: one year, two years and five years before the year of monitoring. Monitoring will be carried out on the basis of data from 4 main sources: from the educational information system, from the information system on science and higher education, from the databases of district examination committees, from the social security system. These sources will provide information on the following: the profession in which the graduate was educated; participation in courses conducted under the education system; study at the next secondary school; study in a specific direction; entry to the examination or vocational examination; obtaining a certificate of maturity, certificates and diplomas; employment and earnings. These data will also assess the situation of school graduates in the labour market With information on the frequency of job changes and the period from school leaving to starting work. The proposed solutions guarantee a high level of protection of graduate personal data. After the combination of data from 4 sources, they will be anonymized immediately. Further monitoring will be carried out only on the basis of non-anonymised data. This solution ensures data security and limits the work on personal data to the minimum necessary. The Ministry of Education and Science will provide non-notified data to the Educational Research Institute by 1 October each year to develop monitoring results. The results of the monitoring will be prepared by the Educational Research Institute annually until 30 November and then published in the Public Information Bulletin of the Ministry of Education and Science. With regard to the anticipated effects of the proposed changes, please, High Izbo, reliable and easily accessible knowledge of graduates' careers will build an image of industry education based on very reliable evidence and data. The education system will respond better to the needs of the labour market. Monitoring will allow for ongoing response to changing socio-economic circumstances. Monitoring will also provide reliable information on the conditions of employment and wages of graduates in particular professions, sectors and regions, allowing students to make more relevant educational and professional decisions. I would very much like to thank the senators of the committee responsible for their very efficient work. And I'm looking forward to any questions. Mrs. Marshal, I'm at your disposal.

In the case of the ELA system, it is sufficient to protect against the identification of the specific persons affected by the indicators presented. The collection of unnecessary, excessive data and the use of data unrelated to the objective is definitely avoided. The first reading of the Senate bill, the Parliamentary printing no. 578, and the Parliamentary print no. 907, took place on 25 February 2021 in the Chamber of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. All the clubs that spoke about both projects claimed that they were worth continuing to process and addressed them to the Committee on Social Policy and the Family. The final result of the work on these two drafts of the Act was that the Committee on Family and Social Policy, as a base project, adopted the parliamentary project rather than the Senate one. The draft I discussed was adopted at the Sejm meeting on 26 February this year. The bill's draft... Well, there's actually, as you say, a bill. Its aim is to improve the life situation of people particularly worthy of the Republic of Poland, and in particular anti-communist opposition activists, politically repressed persons, combatants, victims of war repression and post-war periods, soldiers of military replacement service forced to work in coal mines, quarryings, uranium ore plants and construction battalions, people deported to forced labour and prisoners in labour camps by the Third Reich and the Union of Socialist Soviet Republics, as well as civilian blind victims of war activities. What does this bill provide for these environments? This is very important, because to the whole set of benefits which the Polish State gives its well-deserved, we also add an increase in the income thresholds, which allow for reaching for one-off monetary assistance paid by the head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons. So far it has been possible once every 12 months, after the adoption of this law it will be possible to reach for it once every 6 months. Our combatants and all the well-deserved ones I just mentioned will also receive compensation for all the medicines they need to maintain their health and life, to maintain their well-being. Free medicines will also be given to members of their families. The Act grants these people, above all anti-communist opposition activists and those who are oppressed for political reasons, 5 extra days' leave. Up to 26 working days we add the possibility to take 5 extra days' leave. The amendment of this law makes places... And this was provided for in the parliamentary project. It is about subsidies to places in social assistance homes, which sometimes have to be used by persons mentioned in this law. They bear the cost of living, but add to these costs of staying around... Excuse me, can I have a little water? I felt really bad. I'm so sorry. [[I mean, you can file a sit-down report.]] Excuse me, ladies and gentlemen. Sorry about the mix-up. I'll be right back. I'm so sorry. The point is that the persons I am talking about, those covered by this law, while staying in social assistance homes, are partly paying the cost of their stay. These costs are about 70% their pensions, pensions or social pensions. The remaining part of the costs shall be covered by their closest, entitled or maintenance obligation, or by the municipalities from which they come. We consider it worthwhile, as the Members have said. In order to ensure that part of the costs to be covered by the people or municipalities concerned are covered in full and immediately by the budgets of the municipalities. The municipalities will establish appropriate resolutions and combatants and persons who have the status of an anti-communist opposition activist or who are repressed for political reasons will have a place in such a social assistance house, the said costs will be fully covered by the municipalities. What is most important in this bill Although all these issues that I mentioned earlier are equally important, it is a question of pension. Today, I ask you that pensions paid to those who are repressed, like everyone else in social security, depend on the amount of contributions paid by them to their individual accounts, divided by the number of months of their life that they have left. The problem is that we recognised the period of imprisonment or the time when the person was in so-called. wolf ticket, that is, when she simply could not take up a job for political reasons, for periods included in the internship needed to obtain the right to a pension, but we also considered that this time spent in prison or lack of employment is counted with a zero conversion rate. This was the case until the law entered into force. And both the senators and the Members in their solutions said: no, this is a bad solution, unfair to this environment. The duration of their stay in prison or political repression, because of which these persons either did very low, very low-paid work or simply did not work, must be counted as if they were working, moreover: with a salary of national average, at the time of prohibition of work or in prison.
